But maybe we can implement a specialized active-set LP for the banded covering dual that is much faster. Let's explore.

Primal LP: max 1^T b s.t. A b ≤ M 1, b≥0. A rows k: interval weights f_{k-j} (or f_l). A is nonnegative Toeplitz. Dual: min M 1^T y? Wait primal RHS M, dual: min M ∑ y_k s.t. A^T y ≥ 1, y≥0. (Objective is M times sum y; if we scale y by M, can min M sum y). Actually primal max c^Tb, A_ub b ≤ b_ub=M, then dual min b_ub^T y = M∑y, A^T y ≥ c=1, y≥0. So indeed. Let's revisit direct optimization of P(a) = max c. We can formulate as min t s.t. c_k ≤ t, sum a=1, a≥0. This is nonconvex. However, if we fix active weights y that define h = (y*a), the KKT condition is h constant on support and y nonzero only active. We can iterate:
- Given a, compute c and choose active weights y (e.g., y_k proportional to max(c_k/t -1,0) or hard top).
- Given y, solve for optimal a under KKT? The condition h_i = ∑ y_k a_{k-i} = λ for i in support, y≥0. This is a linear system for a (convolving with y). But also c_k≤t. Maybe solve approximate: Let y fixed. The "eigenvector" problem: minimize max? If we require h constant, we can find a positive solution to y * a = λ 1 (valid convolution). This is linear: (a * y)_i = λ for i=0..n-1. This is a banded linear system. If y positive over enough range, may have unique positive solution. Then normalize. This is reminiscent of SOTA: LP dual gives y satisfying h≥M. Taking h=M and solving for a? This could be a fixed point: a ← positive solution of y*a = constant. If y derived from peaks of a, iterate. This might be elegant and fast.

Maybe simpler: Given y, h = valid(a,y). To make h constant, multiplicative update a_i ← a_i * λ/h_i (or exp) (Sinkhorn). That is dual of row scaling for Hankel matrix. This is exactly nonnegative matrix balancing to make anti-diagonal sums constant. If we set target h_i = current average? The update a_i ← a_i / h_i^α. Thus a generic algorithm:
- choose weights y_k based on current convolution c_k (e.g., inverse of distance to mean, or exp). y≥0.
- compute h_i = valid convolution(a,y) = sum_j a_j y_{i+j}. This is gradient-like.
- update a ← normalize( a * exp( - α * (h_i - h_mean?) ) ) or maybe a * (target/h_i)^β. To flatten h (make constant), use a_i ← a_i / h_i^β. If h_i high, reduce a_i. Since sum normalized, low h increased. This is like making H a closer to constant. For a uniform and central y, h center high -> reduce center. This may reduce c peaks.

What y to choose? If we choose y = c itself? Then h_i = ∑ c_{i+j} a_j. This is related to second derivative? If c high center, h high? y concentrated where peaks high. Might work. But if choose y as indicator of high c, for uniform central h not constant. Good.

Implementation steps for one homologue:
- Given n, initialize a random positive, maybe mix with parent.
- Iterate:
  1. Normalize a sum=1.
  2. Compute c = full convolution of a with a. (using FFT. c length 2n-1. Ensure nonnegative via max(0, real).)
  3. Compute P = max(c). Optional track score=2n*P.
  4. Build weights y_k. Several options:
     - Softmax: t = n*c (or c/P). y = exp((t - max)/τ) / sum. τ controls flatness. If τ small, active top; if large, all. We need y not symmetric. At non-symmetric a, c not symmetric.
     - Hard top K: y_k=1 for c_k > P - δ (δ fraction). Can be singular? If only one center, h constant for its sym? For asymmetric, single active may produce nonconstant h? For a given active k, h_i = a_{k-i}; if a nonconstant, h_i is slice of a, not constant; update a_i ∝ a_i / a_{k-i}^α ~ tends to equalize a_i and reflected a_{k-i}, promoting symmetry around that k! This could make a symmetric, returning to local min? If active k near center and α, may force a symmetric. Maybe not good. Use multiple active.
     - Gap weights: y_k = max(c_k - θ, 0)^p, where θ = P*(1-g) or mean. This includes a band if gaps small.

Multiplicative update with y from peaks:
```
def apply_update(a, y, alpha):
    h = hankel_valid(a,y,N2)
    # floor: if h has zeros, set to floor = 1e-12 maybe relative to mean
    mean_h = h.mean() if h.size else 1
    floor = max(1e-300, 1e-12*max(mean_h,1e-300))
    h = np.maximum(h, floor)
    logh = np.log(h)
    logh -= np.mean(logh)
    # clip to avoid huge
    logf = -alpha * logh
    logf = np.clip(logf, -50, 50)
    a = a * np.exp(logf)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <=0: return uniform
    a /= s
    return a
```

Now, improving to target 1.5. Is multiplicative enough? We can augment with a **coordinate-based "peak shaving"** directly on convolution peaks. For any active peak k, coefficient c_k=∑_{i+j=k}a_i a_j. To reduce it, we can move mass from indices i involved to indices not involved, preserving sum. We can greedily transfer small amounts from high contributors in active peaks to low contributors. This might be effective.

For each peak k, find pairs with high a_i a_j; move mass from i (or j) to positions where c contributions lower. But computing pairs O(n) for each peak. Active peaks maybe few. Could do:
- Choose peak k with max c.
- Compute gradient contribution g_i = a_{k-i} (for i valid). The indices i with high g_i are parts to move: reduce a_i and increase some a_l not contributing to this peak (k-l out of range) or to low-contribution indices.
- To maintain sum, choose donor indices with high g_i and receiver indices with low h? This is like projected subgradient. Multiplicative already does.

Another heuristic: "anti-diagonal balancing" using randomized coordinate updates:
- For each active lag k, target T=current P or average. For each pair in lag, reduce a_i slightly if a_i*a_j large, increase random a_l not in lag. But O(n) per update; okay. But multiplicative smoother might be better.

Let's try to reason about the LP SOTA and maybe implement its b direction via penalty convex solver more effectively, as it is proven to decrease P. The SOTA iteration:
1. Normalize f sum √(2n), M=P.
2. Solve LP max sum b s.t. f*b≤M, b≥0 (or use approximate).
3. g = b * (√(2n)/sum b).
4. f_new = (1-t) f + t g.

Penalty convex solver details:
We want maximize S(b)=∑b subject to c_k = (f*b)_k ≤ M. This is equivalent to minimizing Φ_ρ(b) = -∑b + ρ ∑_k φ((c_k-M)/M), φ(t)=max(0,t)^2. For large ρ, solution feasible.

We can solve with **projected gradient with Barzilai-Borwein** or **FISTA**. Since n=2535, we can use a simple AdaGrad:
```
b = f.copy()
grad_hist = zeros
for it in range(iters):
   c = conv(f,b)
   u = c/M
   z = np.maximum(u-1,0)
   h = hankel_valid(f,z)  # derivative of c wrt b scaled by 1/M
   grad = -1 + 2*rho*h
   # AdaGrad
   step = lr / np.sqrt(grad_hist + grad*grad + eps)
   b = np.maximum(b - grad*step, 0)
   # If b too large? We can normalize? The LP variables b can be up to M/fmin maybe. But if b grows, c maybe >M. Penalty controls.
   if not finite break
```

Let's derive objective and gradient more carefully.
Problem: minimize W(w)=∑_{k=0}^{L-1} w_k + ρ ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} [max(0,1-u_j)]^2, w≥0, where u_j=∑_{l=0}^{n-1} f_l w_{j+l}. f sum1 positive.
Gradient ∂W/∂w_k = 1 + ρ ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} q_j * ∂(1-u_j)^2? q_j = max(0,1-u_j). If u_j<1, (1-u_j)^2 derivative -2(1-u_j)*∂u_j/∂w_k = -2 q_j f_{k-j}. So gradient = 1 - 2ρ ∑ q_j f_{k-j} = 1 -2ρ g_k, where g = f*q (full conv). Good.

def local_search(a, best, time_limit, rng, N1, N2, exact_check=800):

    no_improve=0

Make soft weights:
```
def make_soft_weights(c, tau, rng, noise_amp=0.0):
    cm=c.max()
    if cm<=0: return np.full(c.shape,1/c.size)
    x=c/cm
    exp_arg=(x-1)/tau
    exp_arg=np.clip(exp_arg,-50,0)
    w=np.exp(exp_arg)
    if noise_amp>0:
        # multiplicative random positive
        w *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise_amp, size=c.size))
    w += 1e-15
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: return uniform
    return w/s
```
If tau small, exp underflows floor. Add uniform floor 1e-8 perhaps? This prevents h zero but may bias. Use `w = np.maximum(w, 1e-8 / c.size)` before normalize? For c.size=5069, floor ~2e-12, negligible relative peak 1. Good.

Generate random smooth:
```
def random_smooth(n, rng, low=0, high=5):
    # generate Fourier coefficients random for modes <=high
    x = np.zeros(n)
    max_mode = high
    for m in range(1,max_mode+1):
       amp = rng.uniform(0,1/(abs(m)**0.5))
       phase = rng.uniform(0,2π)
       x += amp*np.cos(2π*m*np.arange(n)/n + phase)
    # add positive base
    x = 1 + 0.5*x; clip positive; normalize
```
Maybe include random mid-high frequency to break symmetry. Use `random_sequence` with gamma: `rng.random(n)**p`? To get nonnegative and variable.

But if initial random too rough, P huge and update maybe slow. Use smooth ramp/cosine better.

Maybe use **logistic ramp** parameter: a_i = (1 + np.exp(β*(x-γ)))/(...) normalized. It transitions from high to low; logistic derivative stable. This is similar exponential but bounded. Continuous logistic might flatten. Let's include.

For x in [0,1], logistic p = 1/(1+exp(-β(x-γ))). Normalized. Sweep β=2,4,8,10 and γ=0.3,0.5,0.7. This can be monotone. Reverse.

Cutoff: clip negative? logistic positive.

Cosine families:
- monotone `1 + A*cos(π*x)` (zero at end if A=1)
- antisymmetric? `1 + A*sin(π*x)`? Has high middle, low ends; may be bad but include.
- U `1 + A*cos(2π*x)` etc with A up to 0.8 to keep positive.

def propose_candidate():
    deadline = time.monotonic()+1780
    rng seed 123
    parent = load...
    n = len(parent) if valid else 2535
    # if n not between 256 and 5000 maybe use fallback
    # Precompute N1,N2.

    best_holder = [inf, None]
    # evaluate exact for each init, update best. Keep top 12 by fast? Actually exact direct for ~40 maybe okay.
    # If parent exact <=2, use as best fallback.
    # For top candidates (score < 1.99 maybe all), run local_search with slice time.
    remaining = deadline - now
    if remaining <=0 return best_seq
    active = inits with finite exact, sorted by score, unique
    # Take top min(8, len)
    chunk = max(30, remaining// (len(active)*?))
    for round in rounds:
       for idx,a in enumerate(active_top):
          if time > deadline: break
          local_best, improved = local_search(a, best_holder, time_limit=chunk, ...)

```python
"""..."""
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def _next_pow2(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<= 1
    return p

def _normalize(a, eps=1e-300):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, eps, None)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= eps or not np.isfinite(s): return np.full_like(a, 1.0/max(1,a.size))
    return a/s

def _clean_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.size ==0: return np.ones(2535)
    return a

def _conv_self(a, N):
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    L = 2*a.size - 1
    c = np.ascontiguousarray(c[:L])
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    s = c.sum()
    if s > 0:
        c *= 1.0/s
    return c

def _cross_h(a, w, N):
    # returns h_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}
    Lw = w.size
    # If a and w same? w length 2*n-1.
    N = max(N, 3*a.size-2)
    ar = a[::-1]
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N) ?
```

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)

    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fw, N)
    h = c[2*a.size-2:a.size-1:-1]

```

Implement covering_y inside local_search:
```
def _covering_y(f, N1, N2, iterations=60, rho=4.0):
    L=2*f.size-1
    logw = np.zeros(L)  # start w=1
    state = np.zeros(L)
    y = None
    for it in range(iterations):
       w = np.exp(logw - np.max(logw))  # unnormalized; scale matters? If we subtract max, objective scale lost. But target 1 in u depends on scale. We need actual w scale. Start logw=0. Use scale parameter? Instead update w directly with multiplicative and then rescale to mean=1 maybe? If we rescale, constraint target changes. But for y weights, scale not matter if we use normalized y; but covering solver needs scale.
```
Scale issue. We can keep `scale = logw.mean()`? Let w = exp(logw) directly; logw can grow but clip. Use `logw = np.clip(logw, -50,50)`, w=exp(logw). Start 0.
Gradient w. Update `logw -= lr*grad/sqrt(state+grad^2)`. This keeps scale. Good.
```
       w = np.exp(logw)
       u = _cross_h(f,w,N2)  # length n, but w length L. h_i=∑ f_j w_{i+j}; yes.
       q = np.maximum(1.0-u,0.0)
       # If there are no violations and objective? break
       g = _conv_full(f,q,N1)  # length L
       grad = 1.0 - 2.0*rho*g
       state += grad*grad
       step = 1.0 / np.sqrt(state + 1e-8)
       logw = logw - step*grad
       logw = np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0)
    w = np.exp(logw)
    w = np.maximum(w, 1e-300)
    return w / w.sum()
```
Will this converge? For uniform f, true covering optimum w constant 1? Starting w=1, u=1, q=0, grad=1, update decreases w. Then u<1, q>0, g positive, grad=1-2ρg. If ρ large, can go negative, w increases. Fixed point maybe w less than1 if finite penalty; y normalized similar uniform? For uniform, y uniform, h uniform, no movement. It may not help uniform. For nonuniform, gives weights. We can use it if available. It costs iterations* (u + g) = 2 conv per iter. If 60 iters, ~120 FFT, okay but if too often heavy. Use when stuck, maybe 20 iters.

But the objective target 1 for f sum1. If f has small min, w may need large. logw clip 50 maybe enough.

Let's draft functions fully:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _clean_seq(x):
    if x is None: return None
    try:
       a=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception: return None
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size==0: return None
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.sum() <=0: return None
    return a
```

`_load_parent`:
```
def _load_parent():
    p=globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    return _clean_seq(p)
```

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a, min_value=1e-14):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a, min_value, None)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= min_value:
        return np.full(a.shape if a.size else (1,), 1.0/max(1,a.size))
    return a/s
```
`a.shape` if scalar? We only use 1D. If a.size 0, return ones(1).

`_next_power2` as above.

`_conv_self`:
```
def _conv_self(a, N):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    L=2*a.size-1
    c=c[:L]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    s=c.sum()
    if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s): c=c*(1.0/s)
    return c
```
`_cross_h`:
```
def _cross_h(a,w,N):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    w=np.asarray(w,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    # N ensure
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)
    h=c[2*n-2:n-1:-1]
    h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
    return h
```
If N not enough? Ensure before call. Use `_ensure_fft(N, needed)`. In local_search pass large enough. In `_cross_h`, maybe if N<3n-2 increase? But reusing N. We can at top:
```
need = 3*a.size - 2
if N < need: N = _next_power2(need)
```
No harm.

`_conv_full_ab`:
```
def _conv_full_ab(a,b,N):
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,N); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,N); L=a.size+b.size-1; return c[:L]
```

`_fast_score`:
```
def _fast_score(a,N):
   c=_conv_self(a,N); return 2.0*a.size*c.max()
```

`_eval_exact` as above.

`_update_holder`:
```
def _update_holder(holder, a):
    seq = np.asarray(a)
    seq = np.where(np.isfinite(seq), seq, 0.0)
    seq = np.clip(seq,0.0,1000.0)
    s=seq.sum()
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    val=_eval_exact(seq.tolist())
    if np.isfinite(val) and val < holder[0]:
       holder[0]=val
       holder[1]=seq.copy()
    return val
```
We should normalize before exact? If sum not 1, score scale invariant, but if sum <0.01 inf. We can normalize in final? For candidate, normalize before update. In local_search we maintain normalized.

`_soft_weights`:
Need rng can be None? We'll always have.
```
def _soft_weights(c, tau, rng, noise_amp=0.0, mix_uniform=0.0):
    m=c.size
    peak=float(np.max(c)) if m else 0.0
    if peak <=0 or not np.isfinite(peak):
        return np.full(m, 1.0/m if m else 1.0)
    x=c/peak
    e=np.clip((x-1.0)/tau, -50.0, 0.0)
    w=np.exp(e)
    if noise_amp > 0:
        w *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise_amp, size=m))
    if mix_uniform > 0:
        w = (1.0 - mix_uniform)*w + mix_uniform*np.ones(m)
    w = w + 1e-14
    s=w.sum()
    if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(m,1.0/m)
    return w/s
```
If tau very small, e underflow all but peak 1, plus noise/uniform floor. Good.

`_apply_alpha`:
```
def _apply_alpha(a, logh, alpha, min_a=1e-14):
    factor = np.exp(np.clip(-alpha*logh, -50.0, 50.0))
    b = a * factor
    b = np.clip(b, min_a, None)
    s=b.sum()
    if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return None
    return b/s
```

`_covering_y`:
```
def _covering_y(f, N1, N2, iterations=40, rho=6.0):
    n=f.size; L=2*n-1
    logw=np.zeros(L)
    state=np.zeros(L)
    for _ in range(iterations):
       w=np.exp(np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0))
       u=_cross_h(f,w,N2)  # length n
       # If u nan, break
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(u)): break
       q=np.maximum(1.0 - u, 0.0)
       # Optional if violations very small:
       if float(np.max(q)) < 1e-7:
           break
       g=_conv_full_ab(f,q,N1)
       grad=1.0 - 2.0*rho*g
       state = state + grad*grad
       step = 1.0 / np.sqrt(state + 1e-8)
       logw = logw - step*grad
       logw = np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0)
    w=np.exp(np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0))
    w=np.maximum(w,1e-300)
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return None
    return w/s
```
This uses N1 for full conv length 2n-1? f and q both length n, full conv length 2n-1; N1≥2n-1. Good.

`_random_init_smooth(n, rng)`:
```
def _random_smooth(n, rng):
    x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    base = np.ones(n)
    max_mode = max(2, min(40, n//10))
    for m in range(1, max_mode+1):
       amp = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.8/np.sqrt(m))
       if amp <=0: continue
       ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
       sign = -1.0 if rng.random()<0.5 else 1.0
       base += sign*amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*m*x + ph)
    base = np.clip(base, 1e-3, None)
    return _normalize(base)
```
Could base become negative clipped; okay.

`_Interp`:
```
def _resample(a, m):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==m: return a.astype(float)
    if m <=1: return np.ones(m)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
    return _normalize(b)
```

def _generate_initials(n, parent, rng):
    inits=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    def add? Can't nested. We'll just inline list.append.
```

Generate:
```
    # parent
    if parent is not None:
       inits.append(_normalize(parent))
       for sig in (0.4,0.8,1.3,2.0):
           v = parent * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=n))
           if v.size != n: v = _resample(v,n)
           inits.append(_normalize(v))
    # uniform
    inits.append(_normalize(np.ones(n)))
    inits.append(_normalize(np.ones(n) + rng.normal(0,0.05,n)))
    inits.append(_normalize(rng.random(n)+1e-3))
    # exponential ramps
    for lam in (0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0,4.0):
       v=np.exp(lam*(x-0.5)); inits.append(_normalize(v))
       inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))
    # power
    pows=(-0.5,0.2,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0)
    for pp in pows:
       for qq in pows:
           v=np.power(x+1e-4, pp)*np.power(1.0-x+1e-4, qq)
           inits.append(_normalize(v))
           if abs(pp-qq)>0.3: inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))
    # logistic
    for beta in (2.0,4.0,8.0,16.0):
       for gamma in (0.25,0.5,0.75):
           v=1.0/(1.0+np.exp(-beta*(x-gamma)))
           inits.append(_normalize(v))
           inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))
    # cos
    for A in (0.3,0.6,0.85,1.0):
       v=1.0 + A*np.cos(np.pi*x)
       inits.append(_normalize(v))
       inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))
       v=1.0 - 0.5*A*np.cos(np.pi*x)
       inits.append(_normalize(v))
       v=1.0 + 0.5*A*np.cos(2*np.pi*x)
       inits.append(_normalize(v))
    # random smooth
    for _r in range(10): inits.append(_random_smooth(n,rng))
    # random ramp with exponent random
    for _r in range(10):
       k=float(rng.uniform(-2,4))
       v=np.power(x+1e-4,k)*np.power(1-x+1e-4,k)
       inits.append(_normalize(v))
    return inits
```

Local_search code more concretely:
```
def _local_search(a, holder, limit, rng, N1, N2, hard_deadline):
    a = _normalize(a)
    n = a.size
    start = time.monotonic()
    c = _conv_self(a, N1)
    local_best = a.copy()
    local_best_score = 2.0*n*c.max()
    _update_holder(holder, local_best)
    alpha = 0.7
    base_tau = float(rng.uniform(0.12, 0.30))
    no_improve = 0
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    covering_counter = 0
    while time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 1.0:
        if time.monotonic() - start > limit: break
        # maybe after 5000 no improve reduce base_tau
        tau = base_tau * (0.998 ** (it // 100))
        tau = max(0.06, min(0.6, tau + 0.03*np.sin(0.07*it)))
        if no_improve > 200 and (it % 50 == 0):
            tau = float(rng.choice([0.06, 0.1, 0.16, 0.25, 0.4]))
        c = _conv_self(a,N1)
        cur_score = 2.0*n*c.max()
        if cur_score < local_best_score - 1e-12:
            local_best_score = cur_score
            local_best = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
        # choose y
        covering_counter -= 1
        if covering_counter <= 0 and no_improve > 300 and stall % 2 == 0 and time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 5:
            y2 = _covering_y(a, N1, N2, iterations=30, rho=8.0)
            if y2 is not None:
                y = (1.0 - 0.35)* y2 + 0.35*_uniform_weights(c.size)
                # But need not recompute c? y length same.
                # set covering_counter=20? To use a few? The covering_y itself already cost; we can just use this y once or for temp.
            else:
                y = _soft_weights(c, tau, rng, 0.03, 0.04)
            covering_counter = 80
        else:
            y = _soft_weights(c, tau, rng, 0.02, 0.03)
        h = _cross_h(a, y, N2)
        meanh = float(np.mean(h)) if h.size else 1.0
        if not np.isfinite(meanh) or meanh <= 0: meanh=1.0
        floor = max(1e-300, 1e-9*meanh)
        h = np.maximum(h, floor)
        logh = np.log(h)
        logh = logh - np.mean(logh)
        # alpha candidates
        cand_alphas = (alpha, 1.5*alpha, 0.5*alpha)
        if no_improve > 100:
            cand_alphas = (alpha, 1.2*alpha, 0.7*alpha, 2.5*alpha)
        best_cand = a
        best_cand_score = cur_score
        for al in cand_alphas:
            if al <= 0.01: continue
            cand = _apply_alpha(a, logh, al)
            if cand is None: continue
            cc = _conv_self(cand, N1)
            sc = 2.0*n*cc.max()
            if sc < best_cand_score - 1e-15:
                best_cand_score = sc
                best_cand = cand
        a = best_cand
        if best_cand_score < cur_score - 1e-15:
            alpha = min(2.5, alpha*1.03)
        else:
            alpha = max(0.2, alpha*0.97)
        # exact eval
        if (it % 600 == 0) or (local_best_score < holder[0]/(2*n) - 1e-8 and it % 50 == 0):
            _update_holder(holder, a)
        # perturb if stuck
        if no_improve > 400 or (no_improve > 200 and (it % 1000 == 900)):
            stall += 1
            mix_best = 0.0 if local_best is None else 1.0
            base = local_best
            if holder[1] is not None and holder[1].size == n:
                base = 0.6*local_best + 0.4*_normalize(holder[1])
            rnd = _random_smooth(n, rng)
            r = 0.15 + 0.25*min(1.0, float(stall))
            if r > 1.0: r = 1.0
            a = _normalize((1.0 - r)*base + r*rnd)
            no_improve = 0
            # maybe randomly reset alpha/tau
            alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.4, 1.5))
            base_tau = float(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.35))
        it += 1
    # final update
    if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 0.2:
        _update_holder(holder, local_best)
    return local_best
```
Need `_uniform_weights` helper? Could just `np.full(L, 1.0/L)` inline. But no lambda. Define:
```
def _uniform_weights(m): return np.full(m, 1.0/m if m else 1.0)
```
In covering blend: `y = 0.65*y2 + 0.35*_uniform_weights(c.size)`.

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

Method:
  - Propose many smooth/parametric random initial profiles of the same length.
  - Run a fixed-length nonnegative mirror-descent style local search.
      * Fast FFT convolution surrogate.
      * Adaptive soft-max weights on the largest convolution coefficients.
      * Multiplicative update a <- a / h^alpha, where h is the weighted
        gradient of the selected peak coefficients.
      * Periodic random re-starts, temperature/alpha sweeps, and occasional
        dual-covering weight suggestions.
  - Validate kept candidates with the exact evaluate_sequence() and always
    return the best exact candidate found before the deadline.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_power2(m):
    p = 1
    m = int(m)
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _clean_sequence(x):
    if x is None:
        return None
    try:
        a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(a.sum()) <= 0.0:
        return None
    return a


def _load_parent():
    p = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    return _clean_sequence(p)


def _normalize(a, min_value=1e-14):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, min_value, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= min_value:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def _resample(a, m):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == int(m):
        return a.copy()
    if a.size == 0 or int(m) <= 1:
        return np.ones(int(m), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(m))
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return _normalize(b)


def _conv_self(a, N):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    L = 2 * a.size - 1
    c = np.maximum(c[:L], 0.0)
    s = float(c.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
        c = c * (1.0 / s)
    return c


def _conv_full_two(a, b, N):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, N)
    L = a.size + b.size - 1
    return c[:L]


def _cross_h(a, w, N):
    """
    Returns h_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}, for i=0..len(a)-1,
    assuming w has length 2*len(a)-1.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    need = 3 * n - 2
    if N < need:
        N = _next_power2(need)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, N)
    # indices 2n-2, 2n-3, ..., n-1
    h = c[2 * n - 2 : n - 2 : -1]
    return np.maximum(h, 0.0)


def _fast_score(a, N):
    c = _conv_self(a, N)
    return 2.0 * a.size * float(c.max())


def _eval_exact(seq):
    try:
        v = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        v = np.inf
    if np.isfinite(v):
        return v
    return np.inf


def _uniform_weights(m):
    if m <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)


def _update_holder(holder, a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    v = _eval_exact(a.tolist())
    if np.isfinite(v) and v < holder[0]:
        holder[0] = v
        holder[1] = a.copy()
    return v


def _soft_weights(c, tau, rng, noise_amp=0.0, mix_uniform=0.0):
    m = c.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    peak = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(peak) or peak <= 0.0:
        return _uniform_weights(m)
    x = c / peak
    e = np.clip((x - 1.0) / tau, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(e)
    if noise_amp > 0.0:
        w *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise_amp, size=m))
    if mix_uniform > 0.0:
        w = (1.0 - mix_uniform) * w + mix_uniform * np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    w = w + 1e-13
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return _uniform_weights(m)
    return w / s


def _apply_alpha(a, logh, alpha, min_a=1e-14):
    factor = np.exp(np.clip(-alpha * logh, -50.0, 50.0))
    b = a * factor
    b = np.clip(b, min_a, None)
    s = float(b.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return b / s


def _covering_y(f, N1, N2, iterations=35, rho=8.0):
    """
    Optional approximate dual covering weights for fixed f.
    It tries to find w >= 0 such that (f*w)_i ~= 1 with small total mass.
    The returned vector is normalized and used only as a soft suggestion.
    """
    n = f.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    logw = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    state = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)

    for _ in range(int(iterations)):
        w = np.exp(np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0))
        u = _cross_h(f, w, N2)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(u)):
            break
        q = np.maximum(1.0 - u, 0.0)
        if float(np.max(q)) < 1e-7:
            break
        g = _conv_full_two(f, q, N1)
        grad = 1.0 - 2.0 * rho * g
        state += grad * grad
        step = 1.0 / np.sqrt(state + 1e-8)
        logw = logw - step * grad
        logw = np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0)

    w = np.exp(np.clip(logw, -50.0, 50.0))
    w = np.maximum(w, 1e-300)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return w / s


def _random_smooth(n, rng):
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    base = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    max_mode = max(2, min(48, n // 8))
    for m in range(1, max_mode + 1):
        amp = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.9 / np.sqrt(float(m)))
        if amp <= 0.0:
            continue
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        sign = -1.0 if rng.random() < 0.5 else 1.0
        base += sign * amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + ph)
    base = np.clip(base, 1e-3, None)
    return _normalize(base)


def _generate_initials(n, parent, rng):
    inits = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    if parent is not None:
        p = _resample(parent, n)
        inits.append(p)
        for sig in (0.35, 0.7, 1.2, 1.8, 2.6):
            v = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=n))
            inits.append(_normalize(v))

    inits.append(_normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))
    inits.append(_normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=n)))
    inits.append(_normalize(rng.random(n) + 1e-3))

    # Exponential ramps.
    for lam in (0.5, 1.0, 1.4, 2.0, 2.8, 4.0):
        v = np.exp(lam * (x - 0.5))
        inits.append(_normalize(v))
        inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))

    # Two-parameter power bending.
    pows = (-0.5, 0.2, 0.6, 1.0, 1.6, 2.4)
    for pp in pows:
        for qq in pows:
            v = np.power(x + 1e-4, pp) * np.power(1.0 - x + 1e-4, qq)
            inits.append(_normalize(v))
            if abs(pp - qq) > 0.35:
                inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))

    # Logistic ramps.
    for beta in (2.0, 4.0, 8.0, 16.0):
        for gamma in (0.25, 0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.75):
            v = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-beta * (x - gamma)))
            inits.append(_normalize(v))
            inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))

    # Simple trigonometric profiles.
    for A in (0.3, 0.6, 0.85, 1.0):
        v = 1.0 + A * np.cos(np.pi * x)
        inits.append(_normalize(v))
        inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))
        v = 1.0 - 0.5 * A * np.cos(np.pi * x)
        inits.append(_normalize(v))
        inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))
        v = 1.0 + 0.5 * A * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
        inits.append(_normalize(v))
        inits.append(_normalize(v[::-1]))

    # Random smooth profiles.
    for _ in range(12):
        inits.append(_random_smooth(n, rng))

    # Random single-exponent profiles.
    for _ in range(10):
        k = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 4.2))
        v = np.power(x + 1e-4, k) * np.power(1.0 - x + 1e-4, k)
        inits.append(_normalize(v))

    return inits


def _local_search(a, holder, limit, rng, N1, N2, hard_deadline):
    a = _normalize(a)
    n = a.size
    start = time.monotonic()

    c = _conv_self(a, N1)
    local_best = a.copy()
    local_best_score = 2.0 * n * float(c.max())
    _update_holder(holder, local_best)

    alpha = 0.7
    base_tau = float(rng.uniform(0.14, 0.32))
    no_improve = 0
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    cover_every = 0

    while True:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > hard_deadline - 1.0 or now - start > limit:
            break

        tau = base_tau * (0.998 ** (it // 100))
        tau += 0.03 * np.sin(0.07 * it)
        tau = max(0.06, min(0.6, float(tau)))
        if no_improve > 220 and (it % 60 == 0):
            tau = float(rng.choice((0.06, 0.10, 0.16, 0.26, 0.42)))

        c = _conv_self(a, N1)
        cur_score = 2.0 * n * float(c.max())

        if cur_score < local_best_score - 1e-12:
            local_best_score = cur_score
            local_best = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        cover_every -= 1
        if cover_every <= 0 and no_improve > 420 and (stall % 3 == 0) and now < hard_deadline - 6.0:
            y2 = _covering_y(a, N1, N2, iterations=28, rho=8.0)
            if y2 is not None:
                y = 0.60 * y2 + 0.40 * _uniform_weights(c.size)
            else:
                y = _soft_weights(c, tau, rng, noise_amp=0.03, mix_uniform=0.04)
            cover_every = 140
        else:
            y = _soft_weights(c, tau, rng, noise_amp=0.02, mix_uniform=0.03)

        h = _cross_h(a, y, N2)
        meanh = float(np.mean(h))
        if not np.isfinite(meanh) or meanh <= 1e-300:
            meanh = 1.0
        floor = max(1e-300, 1e-9 * meanh)
        h = np.maximum(h, floor)
        logh = np.log(h)
        logh = logh - float(np.mean(logh))

        if no_improve > 120:
            alphas = (alpha, 1.3 * alpha, 0.7 * alpha, 2.4 * alpha)
        else:
            alphas = (alpha, 1.5 * alpha, 0.5 * alpha)

        best_cand = a
        best_cand_score = cur_score
        for al in alphas:
            if al <= 0.01:
                continue
            cand = _apply_alpha(a, logh, al)
            if cand is None:
                continue
            cc = _conv_self(cand, N1)
            sc = 2.0 * n * float(cc.max())
            if sc < best_cand_score - 1e-15:
                best_cand_score = sc
                best_cand = cand

        a = best_cand
        if best_cand_score < cur_score - 1e-15:
            alpha = min(2.6, alpha * 1.035)
        else:
            alpha = max(0.18, alpha * 0.965)

        do_exact = (it % 700 == 0)
        if (not do_exact) and np.isfinite(holder[0]):
            do_exact = (local_best_score <= holder[0] / (2.0 * n) + 1e-9) and (it % 60 == 0)
        if do_exact and now < hard_deadline - 2.0:
            _update_holder(holder, a)

        if no_improve > 420 or (no_improve > 250 and (it % 1200 == 1000)):
            stall += 1
            base_seq = local_best
            if holder[1] is not None and holder[1].size == n:
                base_seq = 0.65 * local_best + 0.35 * _normalize(holder[1])
            rnd = _random_smooth(n, rng)
            r = 0.18 + 0.18 * min(2.0, float(stall))
            a = _normalize((1.0 - r) * base_seq + r * rnd)
            no_improve = 0
            alpha = float(rng.uniform(0.45, 1.45))
            base_tau = float(rng.uniform(0.09, 0.36))

        it += 1

    if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 0.3:
        _update_holder(holder, local_best)
    return local_best


def propose_candidate():
    """
    Runs the search for up to about 1790 seconds and returns the best
    exact-evaluated nonnegative sequence found.
    """
    try:
        t0 = time.monotonic()
        deadline = t0 + 1790.0
        rng = np.random.default_rng(20260112)

        parent = _load_parent()
        n = 2535
        if parent is not None:
            if 256 <= parent.size <= 4096:
                n = int(parent.size)
            else:
                parent = _resample(parent, 2535)
                n = 2535
        else:
            parent = _normalize(np.ones(2535, dtype=np.float64))
            n = 2535
            parent = parent

        N1 = _next_power2(2 * n - 1)
        N2 = _next_power2(3 * n - 2)

        holder = [np.inf, None]
        _update_holder(holder, _normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))

        if parent is not None:
            _update_holder(holder, _normalize(parent))

        inits = _generate_initials(n, parent, rng)

        scored = []
        for idx, a in enumerate(inits):
            a = _normalize(a)
            sc = _fast_score(a, N1)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((sc, idx, a))

        scored.sort()

        selected = []
        if scored:
            selected.extend(scored[:20])
            rest = scored[20:]
            if rest:
                selected.extend(rest[:6])

        candidates = []
        for sc, idx, a in selected:
            v = _update_holder(holder, a)
            if np.isfinite(v):
                candidates.append((v, a))

        if not candidates:
            a0 = _normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
            v0 = _update_holder(holder, a0)
            if np.isfinite(v0):
                candidates.append((v0, a0))

        candidates.sort()

        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60.0:
            if holder[1] is not None:
                out = np.asarray(holder[1], dtype=np.float64)
                out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
                out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
                s = float(out.sum())
                if s < 0.01:
                    out = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
                else:
                    out = out / s
                return [float(v) for v in out]
            return [1.0 / n] * n

        top = []
        for v, a in candidates[:10]:
            top.append(a)

        if not top:
            top.append(_normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))

        # First pass: give several diverse starts a limited budget.
        now = time.monotonic()
        per = max(25.0, min(115.0, (deadline - now) * 0.48 / float(len(top))))
        for a in top:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 90.0:
                break
            limit = min(per, deadline - time.monotonic() - 90.0)
            if limit < 4.0:
                break
            _local_search(a, holder, limit, rng, N1, N2, deadline)

        # Second pass: polish the current exact best for a longer time.
        if holder[1] is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            limit = min(700.0, rem * 0.55)
            if limit > 8.0:
                _local_search(holder[1], holder, limit, rng, N1, N2, deadline)

        # Optional: if the current score is still comfortably above target,
        # try a nearby length from the best profile.
        if (
            np.isfinite(holder[0])
            and holder[0] > 1.62
            and holder[1] is not None
            and time.monotonic() < deadline - 45.0
        ):
            if n < 3000:
                n2 = 3072
            else:
                n2 = 2048
            if n2 != n and n2 >= 256 and n2 <= 4096:
                N1b = _next_power2(2 * n2 - 1)
                N2b = _next_power2(3 * n2 - 2)
                a2 = _resample(holder[1], n2)
                rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
                limit = min(180.0, rem * 0.45)
                if limit > 5.0:
                    _local_search(a2, holder, limit, rng, N1b, N2b, deadline)

        if holder[1] is None:
            holder[0] = np.inf
            holder[1] = _normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

        out = np.asarray(holder[1], dtype=np.float64)
        out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
        out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(out.sum())
        if s < 0.01:
            out = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            out = out / s
        return [float(v) for v in out]

    except Exception:
        # Very conservative fallback: uniform sequence.
        m = 2048
        return [1.0 / m] * m
```
